And then I get so behind that I'll just be sitting at my kitchen table crying over it. I've even started forgetting normal stuff, like paying my own bills, which isn't like me at all. I just... I want to make sure I'm handling this okay. Does that make sense? Is that something you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge when you're not working on your art or freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. When I'm not dealing with the business side of things, I actually feel pretty okay. Like, if I'm just watching TV or running errands, I'm totally fine. It's really just when I have to sit down and look at my emails or the invoices that I get all tangled up. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? Like, I'm not just an anxious person all the time, I promise. I try really hard to just be normal when I'm not working. I mean... I'm answering your question right, aren't I? Is it helpful to know that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel this overwhelmed if you were working a traditional 9-to-5 job, rather than running your own freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... I mean, I've had regular jobs before, and I didn't feel this, like, paralyzed by the little things. It really just started when I tried to do this all on my own. \n\nI'm sure if I had a boss telling me what to do, it wouldn't be this hard. I'd just do what they said, you know? But because it's just me, and I'm the one who has to figure it all out... I don't know. I guess I'm just not very good at the business part yet. I'm trying, though! I really am. Do you think that means it's just a phase and I'll get used to it? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about running your own freelance business that you think is causing you the most stress?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think there's just one specific thing, really. I wish I could give you a better answer... I guess it's just all the little administrative things adding up, like the emails and invoices I mentioned earlier. But there isn't some huge, terrible problem or anything. \n\nI feel a little silly even calling it stress, honestly, because I'm really very lucky to be able to try doing art full-time, you know? I shouldn't be complaining at all. It's probably just me overreacting to normal everyday stuff. I hope that's an okay answer? I'm trying to figure it out myself, and I just really don't want to give you the wrong impression.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I want to make sure I'm handling this okay. Does that make sense? Is that something you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge when you're not working on your art or freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. When I'm not dealing with the business side of things, I actually feel pretty okay. Like, if I'm just watching TV or running errands, I'm totally fine. It's really just when I have to sit down and look at my emails or the invoices that I get all tangled up. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? Like, I'm not just an anxious person all the time, I promise. I try really hard to just be normal when I'm not working. I mean... I'm answering your question right, aren't I? Is it helpful to know that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel this overwhelmed if you were working a traditional 9-to-5 job, rather than running your own freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... I mean, I've had regular jobs before, and I didn't feel this, like, paralyzed by the little things. Well, I guess... um, it's really the business side of things. Like, ever since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business, I just... I get so wound up over the invoices and the client messages.

It really just started when I tried to do this all on my own. \n\nI'm sure if I had a boss telling me what to do, it wouldn't be this hard. I'd just do what they said, you know? But because it's just me, and I'm the one who has to figure it all out... I don't know. I guess I'm just not very good at the business part yet. I'm trying, though! I really am. Do you think that means it's just a phase and I'll get used to it? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about running your own freelance business that you think is causing you the most stress?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think there's just one specific thing, really. I wish I could give you a better answer... I guess it's just all the little administrative things adding up, like the emails and invoices I mentioned earlier. But there isn't some huge, terrible problem or anything. \n\nI feel a little silly even calling it stress, honestly, because I'm really very lucky to be able to try doing art full-time, you know? I shouldn't be complaining at all. It's probably just me overreacting to normal everyday stuff. I hope that's an okay answer? I'm trying to figure it out myself, and I just really don't want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain relationships with friends and family due to your current situation with your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, wow... um, I guess I'd feel awful if I just walked away from it. I really don't want you to think I'm a quitter! I'm so lucky to have this opportunity, and I really want to make it work. 

But to answer your question... I suppose I don't actually know if the overwhelmed feelings would completely disappear, mostly because I'm still right in the thick of it all. The community contract and all the changes with setting up the business, they're still actively happening right now. So, um, I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stress of it all—or even just the fallout from it—has been over for months, you know? I haven't had a chance to see what it feels like when it's actually done.

I really do want to stick it out, though. I promise I'm willing to work hard and learn how to handle the admin side. I just need to get a little better at managing it, I think. Is that... does that sound like a normal way to feel about it? I hope I'm answering your question okay.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I don't know if it's the *only* cause, but... yeah, I guess being the one in charge of everything is a big part of it. I mean, I love the actual work, I really do! But having to make every single decision and make sure everything is running smoothly... it's just a lot to carry all by myself. 

I don't want to sound like I'm complaining, though. I know so many people would love to be their own boss, and I'm really grateful for the chance. I just... I think I underestimated how much pressure it would be to not have anyone else to fall back on when things get complicated. 

Do you think that's normal? To feel a little overwhelmed by all the responsibility when you're first starting out? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.

I don't think I worry about that kind of stuff any more than anyone else does. I mean, I care about my health and my family, of course, but it's not like a constant worry or anything. \n\nHonestly, the business stuff is really the only thing that's been on my mind lately. I try to keep everything else pretty balanced and positive. I really don't want to come across as someone who just complains about everything, you know? I feel like I handle normal life things pretty well. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it hard to enjoy the parts of your freelance business that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still really love the creative side of it, you know? I still feel really lucky to be doing what I do. I try to focus on the parts I enjoy and just push through the rest. I really want to make it work, so I try to keep a positive attitude about it. Does that sound like a good approach? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding ungrateful for the opportunity to work for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work-related tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. When I'm just sitting by myself, I'm usually pretty relaxed. I mean, I might think about what I need to do the next day, but it's not like... an anxious feeling, you know? \n\nI actually really like my quiet time. I try to use it to just decompress and, like, read a book or watch a show. I think it's really important to have that downtime so I don't get burnt out. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who can't just sit still and relax. I feel like I'm pretty good at just switching off when I need to. Is that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. I really try to keep my stress under control when I'm not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that your freelance business might fail?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I really worry about it actually failing. I mean, sure, the thought might cross my mind occasionally just because, you know, being a freelancer can be a little unpredictable, but I wouldn't call it a real worry. \n\nI try to stay really optimistic about the whole thing. I figure as long as I keep working hard and doing my best, things will work out, right? I really don't want to be the kind of person who just dwells on the worst-case scenario all the time. I just want to show you that I'm really trying to stay proactive and positive about my situation, even when it gets a little stressful.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a particular event or situation that triggered your feelings of being overwhelmed with your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... um, maybe it started about a month after I took on this big community arts contract? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I wouldn't say they're interfering, no. I mean, I still try to keep up with things I enjoy! I really don't want to just sit around and let this get the better of me. I know how important it is to stay active and positive. 

Sometimes I might be a little tired, or my mind wanders back to the business when I'm trying to relax, but... I'm still doing things. I still try to see my friends and do my hobbies when I can. Well, um... I guess if I really think about it... it's the administrative side of things. I started this because I love making art, and I wanted to turn it into a real business. But... the invoices, and keeping up with client messages... I just get so wound up over them.

I want to make sure I'm handling this okay. Does that make sense? Is that something you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge when you're not working on your art or freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. When I'm not dealing with the business side of things, I actually feel pretty okay. Like, if I'm just watching TV or running errands, I'm totally fine. It's really just when I have to sit down and look at my emails or the invoices that I get all tangled up. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? Like, I'm not just an anxious person all the time, I promise. I try really hard to just be normal when I'm not working. I mean... I'm answering your question right, aren't I? Is it helpful to know that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel this overwhelmed if you were working a traditional 9-to-5 job, rather than running your own freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... I mean, I've had regular jobs before, and I didn't feel this, like, paralyzed by the little things. It really just started when I tried to do this all on my own. \n\nI'm sure if I had a boss telling me what to do, it wouldn't be this hard. I'd just do what they said, you know? But because it's just me, and I'm the one who has to figure it all out... I don't know. I guess I'm just not very good at the business part yet. I'm trying, though! I really am. Do you think that means it's just a phase and I'll get used to it? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about running your own freelance business that you think is causing you the most stress?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think there's just one specific thing, really. I wish I could give you a better answer... I guess it's just all the little administrative things adding up, like the emails and invoices I mentioned earlier. But there isn't some huge, terrible problem or anything. \n\nI feel a little silly even calling it stress, honestly, because I'm really very lucky to be able to try doing art full-time, you know? I shouldn't be complaining at all. It's probably just me overreacting to normal everyday stuff. I hope that's an okay answer? I'm trying to figure it out myself, and I just really don't want to give you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain relationships with friends and family due to your current situation with your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I still see my friends and talk to my mom all the time. I try really hard not to let the business stuff get in the way of, you know, being a good friend or a good daughter. \n\nSometimes I might be a little distracted if I'm thinking about an email I have to send, but I always make sure to show up for them. I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them just because I'm struggling with some paperwork. I try to keep it all separate so I don't bother anyone with it. Is that... is that normal? I hope I'm doing the right thing by not bringing them into it.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: Um, no, my sleep is fine, really. I mean, maybe I toss and turn a little bit if I'm thinking about a deadline, but I'm getting my eight hours. I always make sure to get enough rest so I can be productive the next day. \n\nAnd my appetite is totally normal too. I'm eating just fine. I try to make sure I'm taking care of myself, you know? I wouldn't let myself get run down or anything like that. I really want to make sure I'm staying healthy so I can keep working on the business. Does that sound okay? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping on top of all those basic things. I wouldn't want you to worry about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things beyond just your freelance business, such as your health, relationships, or other everyday concerns?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I worry about that kind of stuff any more than anyone else does. I mean, I care about my health and my family, of course, but it's not like a constant worry or anything. \n\nHonestly, the business stuff is really the only thing that's been on my mind lately. I try to keep everything else pretty balanced and positive. I really don't want to come across as someone who just complains about everything, you know? I feel like I handle normal life things pretty well. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it hard to enjoy the parts of your freelance business that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still really love the creative side of it, you know? Designing and coming up with new ideas is still really fun for me. \n\nI guess... well, maybe the administrative stuff, like the billing and the emails, feels a little bit more like a chore right now, but I think that's normal for anyone running a business, right? I still feel really lucky to be doing what I do. I try to focus on the parts I enjoy and just push through the rest. I really want to make it work, so I try to keep a positive attitude about it. Does that sound like a good approach? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding ungrateful for the opportunity to work for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work-related tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. When I'm just sitting by myself, I'm usually pretty relaxed. I mean, I might think about what I need to do the next day, but it's not like... an anxious feeling, you know? \n\nI actually really like my quiet time. I try to use it to just decompress and, like, read a book or watch a show. I think it's really important to have that downtime so I don't get burnt out. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who can't just sit still and relax. I feel like I'm pretty good at just switching off when I need to. Is that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. I really try to keep my stress under control when I'm not working.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that your freelance business might fail?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I really worry about it actually failing. I mean, sure, the thought might cross my mind occasionally just because, you know, being a freelancer can be a little unpredictable, but I wouldn't call it a real worry. \n\nI try to stay really optimistic about the whole thing. I figure as long as I keep working hard and doing my best, things will work out, right? I really don't want to be the kind of person who just dwells on the worst-case scenario all the time. I try to just focus on the practical steps I can take to succeed. I hope that's a healthy way to look at it? I just want to show you that I'm really trying to stay proactive and positive about my situation, even when it gets a little stressful.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a particular event or situation that triggered your feelings of being overwhelmed with your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... um, maybe it started about a month after I took on this big community arts contract? I was also trying to make the business a bit more, you know, formal at the time. Setting up the LLC, dealing with all the paperwork... it was just a lot all at once. \n\nBut I really don't want it to sound like I'm complaining! It's an amazing opportunity, and I'm so grateful they chose me for the contract. I think I just... maybe I bit off a little more than I could chew right at the start, and that's when I started feeling a bit more stressed and, um, maybe putting off some of the administrative stuff. I'm really trying my best to get a handle on it, though. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for them. Does that make sense? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just making excuses.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your feelings of being overwhelmed would improve if you were able to find a way to better manage the administrative tasks associated with your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. I mean, I think so? If I just had a better system for all the spreadsheets and emails, I'm sure I'd feel a lot more on top of things. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm completely helpless with it or anything! I just... I think getting a little more organized would make a huge difference. I'd love to get better at that part of it so I can just focus on doing a good job for the community arts project. \n\nI'm definitely open to trying whatever you suggest, though. Do you think focusing on the admin side is the right way to go? I really just want to do whatever it takes to get back on track and make sure I'm handling everything the right way.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific task or situation related to your freelance business that you find particularly stressful or overwhelming?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... um, it's really the business side of things. Like, ever since I tried to make my art into a real freelance business, I just... I get so wound up over the invoices and the client messages. \n\nIt's silly, I know. I should just be able to handle it. But I end up avoiding them, and then... well, sometimes it gets bad enough that I'm just sitting at my kitchen table crying, or I end up forgetting to do normal things, like paying my own bills. \n\nI really don't want to sound like I can't handle being an adult, though! It's just that specific part of it that gets to me. I'm sure it's something I can fix with a little more practice, right? I just hope you don't think I'm completely falling apart over a few emails.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your feelings of being overwhelmed would disappear if you were to go back to a traditional job and not have to deal with the administrative tasks of your freelance business?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow... um, I guess I'd feel awful if I just walked away from it. I really don't want you to think I'm a quitter! I'm so lucky to have this opportunity, and I really want to make it work. \n\nBut to answer your question... I suppose I don't actually know if the overwhelmed feelings would completely disappear, mostly because I'm still right in the thick of it all. The community contract and all the changes with setting up the business, they're still actively happening right now. So, um, I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stress of it all—or even just the fallout from it—has been over for months, you know? I haven't had a chance to see what it feels like when it's actually done.\n\nI really do want to stick it out, though. I promise I'm willing to work hard and learn how to handle the admin side. I just need to get a little better at managing it, I think. Is that... does that sound like a normal way to feel about it? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Is it the responsibility of being your own boss that you think is the main cause of your feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't know if it's the *only* cause, but... yeah, I guess being the one in charge of everything is a big part of it. I mean, I love the actual work, I really do! But having to make every single decision and make sure everything is running smoothly... it's just a lot to carry all by myself. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm complaining, though. I know so many people would love to be their own boss, and I'm really grateful for the chance. I just... I think I underestimated how much pressure it would be to not have anyone else to fall back on when things get complicated. \n\nDo you think that's normal? To feel a little overwhelmed by all the responsibility when you're first starting out? I just want to make sure I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.\n\nDoctor: Is it the uncertainty or unpredictability of your freelance business that you think contributes most to your feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I'm not really sure if it's the uncertainty, exactly. I mean, I know freelance work is supposed to be unpredictable, and I really don't want to sound like I can't handle a little bit of the unknown. I knew what I was signing up for! \n\nI think it's more just... the actual day-to-day tasks, like I mentioned before, rather than worrying about what might happen next month. But maybe I haven't really thought about it that way? I hope it's okay that I don't have a perfect answer for that. Do you think the unpredictability is something I should be paying more attention to? I can definitely try to keep an eye on that if you think it would help.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being overwhelmed interfering with your ability to enjoy or participate in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say they're interfering, no. I mean, I still try to keep up with things I enjoy! I really don't want to just sit around and let this get the better of me. I know how important it is to stay active and positive. \n\nSometimes I might be a little tired, or my mind wanders back to the business when I'm trying to relax, but... I'm still doing things. I still try to see my friends and do my hobbies when I can. I hope that's a good sign? I really am trying my best not to let the stress ruin the good parts of my life. Does that sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific aspect of your freelance business that you feel is triggering your overwhelmed feelings more than others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess if I really think about it... it's the administrative side of things. I started this because I love making art, and I wanted to turn it into a real business. But... the invoices, and keeping up with client messages... I just get so wound up over them. \n\nI know I should just handle it, and I really want to be professional! I've even been so distracted by it that I forgot to pay one of my own bills the other day, which is so not like me! \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart, though. I'm trying to get better at managing it. Is it normal to struggle this much with the business side of things when you're just an artist? I know I need to be more resilient overall! \n\nIf I could just fix the admin stuff, I think I'd feel so much better... but honestly, I don't know for absolute certain if it would completely cure everything.
